Poland Strategy

CEE FX performance since Iran war  FX strategy

The zloty has weakened in recent weeks, with €/PLN moving to 4.40 
after trading in a narrow range close to 4.30 in 3Q26. The move 
reflects a less favourable external environment, but also a 
deterioration in Poland’s fiscal outlook.  igher global yields have 
strengthened focus on economies with high deficit and debt 
servicing costs, while rate hikes by both the ECB and Fed have 
narrowed the PLN’s positive rate differential, reducing its carry 
appeal. 
Poland is a glass-half-full or half-empty story. On the positive side, 
GDP growth should continue to outperform the EU, a significant 
inflow of EU funds (RRF) is still ahead and the rising public debt is 
partially offset by very low private debt, so the external balance is 
strong. Also, the PLN could benefit from the conversion of 
substantial EU funds on the market. 
On the negative side, the balance of risks has become less 
favourable. High energy prices, an escalation of Russia’s attacks on 
Ukraine and intensification of hybrid war with Europe remain key 
external threats. At the same time, heightened market scrutiny of 
fiscal positions globally is putting a greater spotlight on Poland's 
large fiscal deficit and rising public debt, while the approaching 
October 2027 elections leave little political appetite for fiscal 
consolidation.  
The cross currents, given both the global and local backdrops, are 
unlikely to support a rapid return to the stronger zloty levels seen 
earlier this year. 
Source: Macrobond  
Poland's general government deficit (% GDP)

Source: Macrobond, ING estimates

2yr, 5yr and 10yr yields (%)  Fixed income strategy

The recent test of 6.5% in 10yr POLGBs was caused by external 
factors, such as the spike in DM yields, as well as the domestic fiscal 
story, although the contribution of the latter is harder to determine. 
US Treasury yields grew amid inflation concerns, higher oil prices 
and fiscal risks, while the Middle East conflict caused another 
energy-driven inflation shock and tighter-for-longer global 
monetary policy stance. In Europe, fiscal concerns are also 
mounting, particularly in France.  
Domestic factors are not supportive for POLGBs either, but much of 
the risk is already priced in, in our view. The PLN yield curve is pricing 
an overly aggressive tightening cycle of around 100bp, while we 
expect the NBP to hike rates by only 50pb (in 1Q27). A repricing of 
market inflation expectations lower should allow POLGBs yields to 
decline, but mostly at the short end.   
Fiscal risks remain the main domestic vulnerability and can 
periodically widen asset swap spreads, but near-term technicals are 
POLGB supportive. Downside rating risk is substantial, given the 
recent deterioration in the fiscal outlook (higher 2022-26 deficits 
and absence of fiscal consolidation in 2027 plan) but is also well 
priced in. On the other hand, strong banking-sector liquidity, 
sizeable EU fund inflows and robust domestic demand should fund 
elevated 2027 borrowing needs. Over the mid-term, a gradual 
global yield compression and resolution of the Iran conflict would 
support a moderate correction in POLGB yields and local fiscal risks 
would limit the downside at the long end, while the dynamics of the 
election campaign remains a wild card. 
Source: Macrobond  
Structure of POLGBs holders

Source: MinFin

Forecast summary   Country strategy: An adjustment that politics could derail 
 2Q26 3Q26F 4Q26F 1Q27F 2Q27F 2026F 2027F 
Real GDP (%YoY) -0.4 -1.2 1.3 1.8 2.5 -0.5 2.2

Macro trend Political cycle Ratings FC LC 
Activity - Presidential: 2029 S&P BBB- BBB- 
Fiscal Tighter Parliamentary: 2028 Moody’s Baa3 Baa3 
Monetary Neutral Local: 2028 Fitch BBB- BBB-

Romania’s macroeconomic adjustment is finally becoming visible, 
but progress remains vulnerable to political uncertainty. The budget 
deficit is narrowing and the country has retained its investment-
grade rating. However, all three major rating agencies continue to 
rate Romania at the lowest investment grade with negative 
outlooks, leaving little room for policy missteps. The political 
stalemate risks overshadowing fiscal progress and keeping the 
sovereign risk premium elevated. With the economy quasi-stagnated 
for almost three years, further consolidation will be difficult. A broad 
political agreement on the 2027-28 fiscal framework would be the 
key positive catalyst, improving policy visibility, supporting the 
rating outlook and allowing fiscal adjustment to translate more 
clearly into lower funding costs and a gradual recovery in confidence. 
At the time of writing (12 October), a new government is expected 
to be installed in the coming days, with a parliamentary 
confidence vote likely on 20 October. 
*Quarterly data is eop, annual is average. Shading denotes ING forecast data  
Source: National sources, ING estimates

GDP (YoY%) and components (ppt)   Consumption is stabilising, but the recovery is fragile

Romania’s economy remains stuck in a low-growth regime. Real 
GDP contracted by 0.8% year-on-year in the first half of 2026 and 
our full-year forecast points to a 0.5% decline. This would extend to 
almost three years the period in which economic activity has 
oscillated around stagnation. The adjustment has been 
concentrated in domestic demand: private consumption subtracted 
around 1.4 percentage points from growth in the first half, while 
both private services and industry weakened. Construction and EU-
funded public investment provided the main cushion. 
The pressure on households has been unusually severe. Retail sales 
fell by more than 6.0% year-on-year in the second quarter of 2026, 
after a 5.2% decline in the first quarter, with weakness broadly 
spread across categories. The drag reflects high inflation, slower 
wage growth, frozen public-sector pay and pensions, and higher 
taxes. More recently, however, the pace of contraction appears to 
have moderated. Our estimate for the third quarter points to a 
smaller decline in retail sales, suggesting that consumption may be 
finding a floor, albeit at a low level. 
We expect GDP growth to recover to 2.2% in 2027, with private 
consumption doing most of the work. This forecast rests on a likely 
return of pension and wage indexation from 1 January, which 
should partially restore households’ real purchasing power 
alongside declining inflation. The assumption is important: weaker 
indexation or additional tax measures would leave the recovery 
more dependent on investment and exports. Conversely, a credible 
political agreement could improve confidence and reduce 
precautionary saving, potentially generating a somewhat stronger 
consumption response. 
EU funds remain the other central source of support. RRF 
implementation has boosted public investment in 2026 and helped 
to offset the contraction in private demand. With the facility now 
ended (though payments are still due to be received by the end of 
2026), the growth impulse will increasingly depend on completing 
projects on time and transitioning smoothly towards cohesion-
policy funding. The end of the RRF is therefore not necessarily a cliff 
edge, but it does change the composition of support. Beyond 2026, 
execution capacity and the ability of public investment to crowd in 
private capital will matter more than the headline allocation. 
Source: NIS, ING estimates

Romania valentin.tataru@ing.com | tiberiu-stefan.posea@ing.com

No end in site for industrial weakness  Low level stabilisation rather than a recovery

Industrial activity continued to deteriorate in 1H26, showing no 
signs of a genuine recovery. Output contracted by 3.8% year-to-
date as weak domestic demand and soft export orders continued to 
limit production. First-half GDP data shows industry subtracting 
around 0.5 percentage points from annual growth, confirming that 
the sector remains a drag rather than a source of momentum. 
At best, the recent data points to a low-level plateau. Energy-
intensive branches still face high costs, and private investment has 
not yet generated a meaningful improvement in capacity or 
productivity. A firmer European cycle and better infrastructure could 
eventually help, but for now we expect little more than stabilisation, 
with a cyclical upswing still not on the radar. 
Source: NSI, ING estimates

Fiscal picture is improving  Fiscal repair is becoming visible

The fiscal correction is visible in the cash data. The January-August 
2026 deficit narrowed to 2.9% of GDP from 4.5% a year earlier, 
supported by stronger tax revenues and nominal restraint on wages 
and pensions. This is a sizeable improvement and keeps the full-year 
target of 6.2% comfortably within reach. The challenge now shifts to 
sustaining the adjustment and making it durable. Political gridlock 
reduces visibility over the 2027 budget and the next phase of 
consolidation. This is precisely where rating risk is concentrated. The 
investment-grade rating has been preserved, but the country is not 
out of the woods. A credible fiscal framework, with clear political 
support, would help entrench the current adjustment and could 
stabilise the rating outlook. Without it, better near-term execution 
may be partly offset by higher funding and currency risk. 
Source: Eurostat, ING estimates

Trade balance (YoY%) and components (ppt)  Weak demand has narrowed the trade gap

The weakness in domestic demand is increasingly visible in the 
external accounts. Lower consumption and weaker private 
investment have reduced imports, narrowing the trade deficit and 
easing the drag from net exports. However, this adjustment reflects 
softer domestic demand more than improved export 
competitiveness or a stronger domestic supply response. Looking 
ahead, a consumption-led recovery in 2027 and a normalisation of 
private investment are likely to revive import demand. Unless 
exports and domestic supply strengthen more decisively, progress 
on narrowing the trade deficit is likely to stall. EU-funded 
infrastructure could lift capacity over time but, in the near term, 
some projects will continue to carry significant import content. The 
external deficit should therefore remain large even as cyclical 
pressures ease. Source: NSI, ING estimates

Inflation (%YoY) and main components (ppt)  Inflation is easing but the story remains uncomfortable

Inflation has eased, but its composition remains unfavourable. The 
sharp decline that started in July was largely driven by base effects 
rather than broad-based disinflation. Energy and food prices remain 
a source of upside risk. Underlying inflationary pressures remain 
persistent. Services inflation continues to reflect past wage 
increases and elevated labour costs, while compressed margins 
may be limiting firms’ ability to absorb cost pressures. Exchange-
rate depreciation also remains a risk, given inflation expectations 
have stayed above target for several years. The combination of 
weak activity and sticky domestic inflation remains a key feature of 
the current macroeconomic picture. 
Source: NIS, ING estimates

Romania Strategy

Gradual RON weakening seems back in fashion   FX strategy (with Frantisek Taborsky, EMEA FX & FI Strategist)

The Romanian leu has experienced unprecedented volatility in 
recent weeks, and overall, the RON has significantly 
underperformed its CEE peers this year. Although the fiscal situation 
appears under control for now, political uncertainty and an unclear 
outlook for the coming years are fuelling market concerns regarding 
potential rating downgrades and the loss of investment-grade 
status. 
The EUR/RON exchange rate jumped from around 5.250 in early 
September to its current position just below 5.350. While the market 
is attempting to stabilise, its future direction depends heavily on 
domestic political developments. Although the full data set for 
September is not yet available, a significant drop in banking sector 
liquidity, reflected in a spike in FX implied yields, is expected. The six-
month FX implied yield touched 7.00% in early October, signalling a 
tightening of monetary conditions. However, we have since seen 
some easing, with FX implied yields returning close to pre-spike 
levels.  
The NBR’s FX reserves stood at €72bn at the end of September, close 
to record highs. Consequently, we believe the central bank will 
continue to smooth out EUR/RON fluctuations without managing to 
fully prevent further upward movement should market pressures 
arise. 
We forecast the EUR/RON exchange rate at 5.350 by the end of this 
year and 5.450 by the end of next year. 
Source: NBR, ECB, ING  
Liquidity position of the local banking sector

Source: NBR, Macrobond, ING estimates

10y ROMGBs spreads vs CEE3 (bp)  Fixed income strategy (with Frantisek Taborsky, EMEA FX & FI

Strategist and James Wilson, EM Sovereign Strategist) 
As with the FX market, ROMGBs have faced selling pressure in recent 
weeks; however, valuations have not changed materially relative to 
CEE peers and we continue to view ROMGBs as expensive. The 
spread against Polish government bonds (POLGBs) has tightened to 
below 100bp, the narrowest level since a brief spell in mid-2023. 
Funding for ROMGBs is proceeding according to plan, with the 
funding programme roughly 75% complete, and fiscal execution 
points to a better-than-expected outcome for the year. 
Nevertheless, the primary driver right now is the future policy 
trajectory, just as it is for FX. Given the tight spreads relative to 
peers, we see a risk of a bearish shift and further spread widening 
from here. 
In the Eurobond space, the recent rating affirmation by S&P should 
offer some relief while clearer progress towards government 
formation would offer another potential positive catalyst. However, 
in the near term, resolution of the current political crisis is likely to 
prompt new issuance in the Eurobond space, acting as a technical 
headwind. Beyond this, the medium-term outlook will depend 
largely on the fiscal direction of any new government, with the task 
of further consolidation now appearing more challenging given 
political polarisation and a tough external environment. We would 
prefer to wait for clarity on the 2027 budget before becoming more 
structurally bullish, with the recent rebound having taken spreads 
back towards the tighter end of the YTD range versus similarly rated 
peers. 
Source: Refinitiv

Source: Refinitiv, ING

Macro trend Political cycle Ratings FC LC 
Activity + Presidential: 2026 S&P BBB- BBB- 
Fiscal Stimulative Parliamentary: 2026 Moody’s Ba2 Ba2 
Monetary Restrictive Local: 2028 Fitch BB+ BB+

Serbia’s economy has remained resilient despite elevated domestic 
and external uncertainty, supported by robust consumption, a 
strong labour market and continued investment ahead of EXPO 
2027. The more supportive fiscal stance, including higher social 
spending, should sustain near-term demand but could add to both 
inflation and external pressures. The unresolved NIS ownership issue 
remains the main energy risk, as the US Treasury’s extension lasts 
only until 30 October. Meanwhile, the 25 October parliamentary 
election and Aleksandar Vučić’s pursuit of the premiership add to 
the near-term political uncertainty, while Serbia’s long-term 
balancing of relations with the EU, Russia and China has become 
increasingly difficult against the current geopolitical backdrop. 
*Quarterly data is eop, annual is average; shading denotes ING forecast data 
Source: National sources, ING estimates

Real GDP (%YoY) and contributions (ppt)  Growth strengthens, but not without trade-offs

Economic activity strengthened during the first half of 2026, with 
real GDP growth accelerating to 3.8% year-on-year in the second 
quarter. Household consumption remained supported by rapid 
wage growth and a resilient labour market, while infrastructure 
investment linked to EXPO 2027 continued to underpin construction 
and domestic demand. Nevertheless, the increasingly import-
intensive investment cycle is weighing on the external balance, 
while foreign direct investment remains below its 2024 peak. 
Growth should remain relatively solid, although the outlook will 
depend on political normalisation, external demand and a durable 
resolution of the NIS refinery issue. 
Source: Eurostat, ING estimates

FX stability remains the main policy tool  FX stability to persist; rate cuts delayed

Inflation accelerated to 4.1% in September, returning close to the 
upper end of the NBS target band and highlighting continued 
uncertainty around food and energy prices. Against this backdrop, 
the central bank kept the key rate unchanged at 5.75% in October, 
making near-term monetary easing less likely. Meanwhile, EUR/RSD 
has remained broadly stable within the 117.3–117.5 range, 
supported by official interventions. The NBS sold €475m between 
January and September to maintain currency stability. We expect 
the NBS to maintain a firm grip on the dinar going forwards. 
Source: NBS, ING estimates

SERBIA USD credit spreads (bp)  Sovereign credit: Stable fundamentals vs political noise

Despite some deterioration in recent years (wider fiscal deficit, drop 
off in F  ), Serbia’s fundamentals remain closer to higher-rated IG 
peers, and generally even stronger than most in the CEE region. The 
focus remains on pre-election fiscal loosening and energy pressures 
on the external balance. However, politics remains the point of 
uncertainty, with upcoming elections bringing, at a minimum, the 
potential for further anti-government protests and greater 
uncertainty about the outcome than at any time in the past 
decade. We would therefore maintain a favourable fundamental 
view on the sovereign, but reduce some exposure ahead of 
elections.  
Source: Refinitiv, ING   James Wilson, EM Sovereign Strategist

Macro trend Political cycle Ratings FC LC 
Activity – Presidential: May 2028 S&P BB- BB- 
Fiscal Tight Parliamentary: May 2028 Moody’s Ba3 Ba3 
Monetary Tight Local: March 2029 Fitch BB- BB-

The Middle East remains focused on the US-Iran standoff. In this 
environment, pressure on energy prices persists, creating challenges 
for net energy-importing economies such as Turkey. Domestically, 
the closure of certain investment funds prompted some investors to 
unwind money market fund positions, although dollarization 
pressures remained contained. CBT reserves declined in September 
amid weaker confidence, while maintaining strong reserve buffers 
remains important for absorbing potential shocks and preserving 
the exchange rate as a key policy anchor. Slower economic activity, 
softer domestic demand, low core goods inflation and easing 
services inflation have reduced upside inflation risks, leading the CBT 
to lower the effective funding cost. Markets continue to expect 
further rate cuts this year, although reserve volatility and investor 
confidence remain important considerations for the CBT. *Quarterly data is eop, annual is average Source: National sources, ING estimates

GDP growth (% YoY)  Macro digest

Elevated borrowing costs and macroprudential measures, which 
were tightened further in May, appear to be contributing to a more 
pronounced slowdown in second-quarter 2026 GDP growth. 
Domestic demand, particularly private consumption, has continued 
to lose momentum, making its weakest contribution since 2024 as 
spending adjusts to tighter financial conditions. At the same time, 
net exports have returned to positive territory and are providing 
support to overall economic activity. On a sequential basis, growth 
was held back by weaker private and government consumption, 
although this was largely offset by a strong contribution from net 
exports. The economy is on a moderating growth path in the second 
half of this year; however, the recent disruption in investment funds 
is likely to place additional pressure on domestic demand, 
increasing downside risks to our full-year GDP growth forecast of 
2.8%. Compared with the previous plan, the new 2027-2029 
Medium-Term Plan (MTP) projects a growth composition with a 
larger contribution from private consumption, a significantly higher 
inflation trajectory, stronger primary spending and a wider current 
account deficit next year. These projections come ahead of 
elections that, according to signals from the government bloc, are 
likely to be held in early 2028. Against this backdrop, GDP growth is 
expected to accelerate to above 4% next year. A potential 
resolution of the Gulf conflict could support growth prospects, while 
any further escalation would add to already high downside risks.  
Lower-than-expected September CPI contributed to a further 
decline in annual inflation. Given still high energy prices and 
unanchored inflation expectations, we see end-2026 inflation at 
29.0% though risks are on the downside with weakening demand. 
The latest MTP revised the 2027 inflation forecast upwards to 21%, 
compared with 9% in the previous plan. The new projection is 
broadly in line with the 21.9% consensus in the Market Participants 
Survey. The upwards revision to the 2027 forecast was driven partly 
by the carry-over impact of higher-than-expected inflation this year 
on the following year’s starting point, and partly by the global 
economic backdrop. According to CBT calculations, the impact of the 
US-Iran war and related global developments on 2026 inflation is 
estimated at around 7ppt. After eliminating war related factors, 
inflation would be below 25%. The new 2027 inflation forecast 
indicates that the disinflation process has been pushed back further 
in the current stabilisation programme, in place since mid-2023. 
Uncertainty surrounding oil prices - along with their spillover effects 
on other commodity prices - continues to pose risks to the inflation 
outlook as well as to the expected fiscal impulse envisaged in the 
MTP. Accordingly, we forecast inflation next year at 25%, with risks 
on the upside.   
Source: TurkStat, ING

Directional Economics CEEMEA – Central Europe’s new horizons: from investment hosts to global investors October 2026

Turkey 16Bmuhammet.mercan@ing.com.tr

Unemployment vs NPLs (%)  Labour market stability conceals rising pressures

The seasonally adjusted unemployment rate has remained broadly 
stable in the 8-9% range since mid-2024, occasionally falling below 
8% and reaching 7.8% in August 2026. As job creation has been 
largely flat, the relatively low unemployment rate mainly reflects a 
gradual decline in labour force participation from nearly 54% to 
52.5%. Broader labour market indicators suggest increasing strain, 
with the underutilisation rate, which includes the unemployed, the 
potential labour force and time-related underemployment, rising to 
31.0%, close to an historic peak. Meanwhile, asset quality is 
gradually weakening, largely due to consumer lending. Although 
the overall NPL ratio remains historically low, rising GPL ratios 
warrant caution and may require further restructurings, potentially 
accelerated by recent fund-related stress. 
Source: TurkStat, BRSA, ING

Breakdown of C/A financing (12m-rolling, US$bn)  External risks are still alive

The current account deficit has been on a widening trend, while 
capital flows have remained volatile recently. The trajectory of the 
current account balance is expected to be influenced by a mix of 
external risks alongside domestic demand conditions. In this regard, 
geopolitical shocks increase upside pressures on the deficit given 
the elevated trajectory of oil and gas prices. The signing of an 
agreement between the US and Iran, on the other hand, should be 
supportive with easing pressure on energy prices. The government 
forecasts a current account deficit of 2.6% of GDP in 2026, followed 
by a gradual improvement in subsequent years. While the new 
projections are higher than those in the previous plan, the elevated 
and gradually increasing growth path outlined in the MTP raises 
doubts over whether a narrowing of the current account deficit can 
be achieved. Source: CBT, ING

Primary balance (12m-rolling, % of GDP)  Fiscal outlook faces energy-related pressures

The budget deficit has remained below the government’s target. 
However, the fiscal burden from energy subsidies is expected to 
continue in the near term as elevated global energy prices remain a 
challenge. We forecast a 2026 budget deficit of 3.3% of GDP, with 
lower capital expenditure and resilient tax revenue partly offsetting 
subsidy costs. The MTP projects a central government deficit of 3.1% 
of GDP, rising to 3.5% in 2027 before declining to 2.8% by 2029. 
Although most earthquake-related reconstruction spending has 
been completed, total spending is expected to rise from 23.0% to 
24.5% of GDP, mainly due to higher current transfers, reserve 
allocations and interest payments. Stronger revenue collection, 
equating to around 1% of GDP, should provide a partial offset, while 
fiscal policy is intended to support disinflation. Source: Ministry of Treasury and Finance, ING

Banking sector volume expansion  CBT’s targeted support for liquidity and  MEs

The CBT has introduced targeted measures to support market liquidity 
and SME lending. The SME loan growth limit was increased from 
4.5% to 5.0%, reversing the tightening implemented on 23 May. The 
Bank also lowered the share of TRY required reserves held in blocked 
accounts, giving banks greater flexibility in liquidity management 
without changing overall reserve requirements. These steps are 
designed to address temporary liquidity and credit pressures rather 
than signal a broader easing of monetary policy. Meanwhile, loan 
growth has slowed due to the macroprudential measures 
introduced in May and the continued tight policy stance. TRY-
denominated loans remain the main driver of commercial lending 
because of stricter FX lending limits, while weaker momentum in 
TRY SME lending has widened the gap with non-SME lending. Source: BRSA, ING

Turkey Strategy

FX: Spot vs forward and INGF  FX strategy (with Frantisek Taborsky, EMEA FX & FI Strategist)

Fund volatility has had little impact on the dollarization ratio for 
deposits and mutual funds. TRY funds recorded outflows of around 
TRY580bn between end-August and 14 October as some investors 
seemed to be unwinding their money market fund holdings (the 
amount excludes price effects and funds for which trading was 
suspended, though it has been hard to fully track activity since 
15 September as some funds shifted to monthly valuations). On the 
other hand, we estimate that outflows in FX- and gold-linked assets 
held in funds stood at US$2.8bn. Recent increase in TRY deposits and 
repos suggests that dollarization remains contained.  
Following more than US$30bn net FX purchases between June and 
August, the CBT’s net FX sales came in at US$8.5bn between 
1 September and 9 October. We see US$2.0bn net portfolio 
outflows, US$3.0bn swap outflows, US$3.1bn increase in residents’ 
FX-linked assets (netted across banks and funds), and a US$1.1bn 
increase in residents’ gold deposits (parity-adjusted), which leaves 
us US$4.3bn to account for. Hence, we think roughly half of the net 
FX sales were related to residents’ FX demand in this period with 
corporates having a higher weight. While the CBT recently adjusted 
the pace of FX depreciation higher, it is likely a mechanical move 
rather than a shift in FX policy.  
We continue to favour TRY for carry trades, anticipating that the 
carry return will continue to outweigh depreciation.  
Source: Bloomberg, ING estimates  
CBT funding

Source: CBT, ING

Yield curve (%)  Fixed income strategy (with Frantisek Taborsky, EMEA FX & FI

Strategist; James Wilson, EM Sovereign Strategist) 
Given the September CPI outcome and weakness in domestic 
demand, we see the policy rate at 35% by the end of 2026 following 
two 100bp cuts, at the October and December MPC meetings. The 
CBT also started purchase auctions on 20 August and, by mid-
October, had purchased TRY117.8bn (nominal). According to the 
Bank, MMFs hold a significant amount of TLREF-linked instruments, 
which has led them to accelerate bond purchases. Continuation of 
purchases should also be supportive for bond yields. 
The market is pricing in a CBT rate path for this year that is nearly 
identical to ours but remains significantly more hawkish regarding 
next year, pricing in a final rate cut that would subsequently be 
reversed, leaving the rate unchanged. With growing conviction that 
the disinflationary process will continue, we see room for further 
curve steepening and lower front-end, while the long end remains 
elevated amid global uncertainty. 
In the Eurobond space, Turkey has held up well despite the global 
macro volatility in recent months and local investment fund noise. 
We continue to see fundamentals as relatively stable against a 
backdrop of higher energy prices and global bond yields. In this 
context, a decent pickup of 30-40bp over the BB sovereign average 
remains attractive, despite some tightening in recent weeks.  
Source: Bloomberg, ING estimates

Source: Treasury, CBT, ING

The hryvnia remained under pressure as the dollar strengthened, 
partly due to renewed Middle East tensions. Consequently, USD/UAH 
temporarily broke above 45.0, reaching its highest level on record. 
Hryvnia depreciation could have been sharper, but NBU actions 
provided support. In September, the central bank raised rates by 
50bp to 16.0% and maintained sizeable FX interventions, with the 
four-week average close to record highs, which demonstrates the 
NBU’s determination to stabilise the exchange rate. FX reserves 
remain volatile but new tranches of foreign loans should lift them to 
around US$60bn. The macroeconomic environment remains 
challenging due to the ongoing war with Russia, despite intensified 
rhetoric and pressure from the US administration. 
*Quarterly data is eop, annual is average. Shading denotes ING forecast data 
Source: National sources, ING estimates

GDP growth (%)  Economic stagnation in 2026 due to war disruptions

n 2026, Ukraine’s G P level seems set to remain 20% lower than in 
2021, prior to Russia’s full-scale invasion in February 2022. The 
economy has nevertheless shown considerable resilience during the 
war. However, the intensification of attacks on energy, warehousing 
and logistics infrastructure has led to severe supply-side disruptions 
compounded by labour shortages, weighing on GDP growth, which 
slowed to 1.8% in 2025 and is expected to fall below 1% in 2026. 
The recent acceleration in inflation has added to these pressures by 
eroding real incomes and private consumption.  
Assuming hostilities continue into 2027, economic activity is likely to 
rebound only gradually with energy shortages posing an additional 
risk during the winter months. The geopolitical outlook remains 
highly uncertain with little prospect of an imminent end to the war 
despite intensified diplomatic efforts. 
Source: NSI, ING

Inflation and NBU policy rate (%)  Return of double-digit inflation may require NBU rate hikes

Price pressures have increased in recent months as a consequence 
of intensified Russian attacks on energy, logistics and civil 
infrastructure and the war in the Middle East (global oil shock). CPI 
inflation accelerated from 8.1% YoY in August to 10.0% YoY in 
September, well above expectations. Monthly inflation of 2.1% MoM 
was the highest since 2015. The surge in inflation was broad-based, 
and included not only energy and food categories, but also core 
inflation, which reached 8.8% YoY in September, up from 8.1% YoY 
in August. Higher food prices surprised despite the relatively robust 
harvest of grains, fruits and vegetables. War-related disruptions and 
unfavourable global trends may further fuel inflationary pressures 
and require additional NBU policy tightening in 2026 and a 
restrictive policy stance in 2027 to contain second-round effects. 
Source: NBU, ING

Fiscal and external current account balances (% of GDP)  Persistent fiscal gap funded mainly by EU loans in 2026-27

To finance the war with Russia, Ukraine has mobilised substantial 
internal resources while relying heavily on external financial 
assistance in the form of foreign loans from international partners. 
This includes the €90bn EU loan due to be disbursed in 2026-27, 
which comprises both defence and budget-support components. 
This has translated into fast rising public and external debt levels, 
both of which exceeded 100% of GDP in 2025, and are set to rise 
further in the coming years.  
Both fiscal and external current account gaps, as well as public and 
external debt levels, are set to widen significantly in the coming 
years. In July, the NBU projected foreign assistance inflows at 
US$87bn in 2026, US$59 in 2027, and US$33bn in 2028. Source: Ministry of Finance, NBU, IMF, WB, ING

Uzbekistan remains our preferred story in the CCA region, combining 
strong and diversified growth, continued reforms and positive 
sovereign-credit momentum. An expected catch-up in gold exports, 
alongside ongoing capital inflows linked to the privatisation pipeline 
(UzNIF+12 SOEs) is supporting a positive performance from the 
soum. Combined with fiscal consolidation, this has helped bring the 
inflation rate down since the start of the year, improving the scope 
for further monetary easing. At the same time, longer-term 
vulnerabilities stem from persisting twin deficits, continued high 
state involvement in the economy and a long history of above-
target inflation. A more meaningful monetary easing and a lasting 
break from UZS’s depreciation trend would require a further decline 
in inflation and more durable private capital inflows. 
*Quarterly data is eop, annual is average. Shading denotes ING forecast data 
Source: National sources, ING estimates

GDP growth by sectors (% YoY, ppt)  Activity: Accelerating beyond potential

Uzbekistan’s G P growth has been exceptionally strong recently, 
supported by domestic demand catching up from a low base. While 
activity is expected to moderate from the current above-potential 
levels, Uzbekistan should remain one of the fastest-growing 
economies in emerging markets. Strong investment spending, 
industrial activity and ongoing reforms continue to support 
expansion, while positive demographic trends provide an additional 
tailwind. A gradual slowdown in the GDP growth is likely to reflect 
normalisation in the activity trends, with growth remaining 
comfortably above regional peers. Uzbekistan's challenge is 
increasingly one of managing overheating pressures rather than 
finding additional sources growth in consumption and investment. 
Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Inflation, key rate, and CPI expectations (%)  Monetary policy: Scope for gradual easing

Over the past couple of years, inflation in Uzbekistan has moderated 
substantially from previous highs, aided by the recent fiscal 
consolidation, and this has created room for a cautious monetary 
policy easing cycle. However, current CPI remains above the central 
bank's long-term objective, while strong domestic demand, 
administrative tariff adjustments and global food and commodity 
risks argue against aggressive rate cuts. We therefore expect the 
central bank to deliver only gradual easing from a still-restrictive 
level. Real rates remain relatively attractive and should continue to 
support macro stability while allowing policymakers to balance 
growth objectives against the need for further disinflation. Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  
/ 
Balance of payments composition (US$bn)  External balance: Gold and capital inflows support UZS

Our stronger-than-consensus medium-term view on the soum 
continues to be reinforced by recent developments. High real rates, 
gold prices, an expected catch-up in physical gold exports and 
growing foreign participation in Uzbekistan's privatisation 
programme provide important support for the balance of payments 
and FX market. This should underpin the resilience of the UZS 
despite the country's persistent trade and current-account deficits. 
However, the longer-term outlook is less certain, as Uzbekistan 
continues to run sizeable twin deficits, while inflation remains above 
target. As a result, we view the UZS positively in the near term but 
believe a durable break from the historical depreciation trend would 
require sustained progress in reducing macro imbalances and 
deepening structural reforms. 
Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES
Credit volumes have temporarily cushioned the rise in interest rates

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

Document 355: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Bond Yields Ease

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond Yields Ease
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond Yields Ease

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond
Yields Ease
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 25 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Jeffrey Hamilton / Unsplash

Bond yields eased in most major markets last week, but France was the
glaring exception. In stocks, the global AI selloff kept hitting the equities
markets that had gained the most this year, putting the US back ahead of
the All Country World ex-US ytd. That supports our downgrade last week
of Go Global to market weight, though the fundamental case for overseas
stocks remains strong. Here's more:
I. Global Bonds

### Page 2

Yields fell in five of the eight major bond markets we track (chart). The US
10-year Treasury yield fell to 5.00%. France's yield jumped 24bps to
5.02%, slightly surpassing the US’s. Yields also fell in the UK, Australia,
Germany, and Japan.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields widened 31bps
last week to 167.9bps (chart). Last week, we wrote that contagion from
France had eased for now. It’s back. Italy’s spread widened to 123.9bps

from 106.6bps. Greece’s widened to 105.0bps from 90.8bps. Italy’s 10-
year bond now yields 44bps less than France’s.
II. Global Stocks
Brazil remains at the top of the country ETF rankings mtd, up 14.3% in
dollars (chart). South Korea remains at the bottom, down 17.2%. The US

### Page 3

is down just 0.3%, while the All Country World ex-US ETF is down 2.8%.
Go Global no longer leads Stay Home this year. The US ETF is up 11.4%
ytd, ahead of the All Country World ex-US ETF's 9.5% (chart). Taiwan
(65.1%) and South Korea (55.6%) still top the ytd rankings, followed by
Brazil (34.1%). China (-15.6%) and Indonesia (-43.3%) are at the bottom.

### Page 4

III. Global Earnings & Valuations
The US MSCI's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has
edged up to 65.0% (chart). The Emerging Markets (EM) MSCI’s share
slipped to 11.7% from 12.1% last week.
Yet the EM MSCI now accounts for 20.2% of the All Country World MSCI’s
forward earnings (chart). That’s more than Europe’s 15.0%. It’s the first

### Page 5

time that EM has overtaken Europe since the data began in 1995. The US
accounts for 54.4% of forward earnings, well below its market-cap share.
That mismatch shows up in valuations. The US MSCI’s forward P/E is
19.2 versus 12.2 for the All Country World ex-US (chart). The ex-US
multiple has dropped from 15.7 in January even as its forward earnings
soared. The EM MSCI trades at just 9.0.
IV. In Focus
(1) Brazil. Brazil’s MSCI stock price index is up 22.2% ytd in local

currency and 31.0% in dollars (chart). The real has gained 8.0% against
the dollar this year. In dollar terms, the index remains less than half its
2008 peak. Stocks rallied after Senator Flávio Bolsonaro took a surprise
lead in the October 4 first round. He held a narrow edge in the final polls

### Page 6

before the October 25 runoff against President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva. A
Bloomberg poll released on October 19 gave Bolsonaro 51.1% of valid
votes against 48.9% for Lula.
Valuations aren’t stretched. Brazil’s forward P/E is 9.2 (chart). That’s
below its 2025 high of 10.7. The catch is that analysts expect earnings to
grow 44.6% this year but just 0.6% in 2027.
Brazil’s central bank has room to ease. It has lowered the Selic rate to
13.75% from a peak of 15.00% (chart). Inflation was 4.6% in September.

That leaves a real policy rate of about 9%.

### Page 7

(2) China. China’s property bust is deepening. The Shenzhen Real Estate
Stock Price Index fell to 953.55, breaking below the floor that had held
since 2012 (chart). Last week, we wrote that we would worry if it did that.
Now we are worried. Real estate investment is down 20.6% through the
first nine months of the year. Exports continue to carry the economy.
Beijing’s stabilization efforts haven’t stopped the selling. The China ETF
(FXI) fell 4.5% last week. Chinese technology stocks have missed the AI

rally that lifted US tech (chart). The China Technology ETF (CQQQ) is at
40.26, far below its 2021 peak.

### Page 8

China’s bond market confirms the deflationary picture. The 10-year
government bond yield is down to 1.62% (chart). It’s one of only two major
yields that have fallen this year. China’s yield is now well below Japan’s
2.80%.

Document 412: Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Quantum dictionary, part 1_ Decoding the fundamentals From superposition to decoherence, we unpack the concepts and technologies shaping the future of quantum computing. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

13030420     1

Transformation 
Quantum dictionary, part 1: Decoding the fundamentals 
21 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• What is quantum computing? How does it work? What is a qubit? And when will quantum reach its full potential? Quantum 
computing challenges our intuition and our understanding of what computers can do. In this two-part dictionary series, we 
decode key terms behind what could be the next major technological revolution. 
• Qubits are the basic building blocks of quantum computers, harnessing properties such as superposition and entanglement to 
process information. However, qubits are highly sensitive to their surroundings where disturbances can lead to errors in 
quantum computation, making error correction a key challenge for building quantum computers. 
• There is no single approach to building a quantum computer. Researchers and companies are pursuing a range of qubit 
technologies, from neutral atoms and photons to spin, superconducting, topological and trapped-ion qubits, while quantum 
computing itself is likely to increasingly work alongside classical computing and AI.

First, let’s lay the groundwork… 
Quantum computing  
Quantum computing uses qubits and quantum properties such as superposition (defined later on) to perform complex 
calculations and solve certain problems faster than classical computers.1 From a practical perspective, quantum computing could 
be used to tackle problems related to optimization, machine learning, simulation and factorization across a range of sectors, 
including autos and logistics, financial services, energy and climate, health and life sciences and national security. 
Current quantum computers can perform ‘just’ thousands of quantum operations. As the technology advances from thousands to 
millions, billions and eventually trillions of operations, quantum computers could begin to have commercial applications.  
• Millions: Financial services and security could experience some of the first use cases, such as threat detection, fraud 
prevention, derivatives pricing, bond trading and arbitrage. 
• Billions: Quantum could help power AI through advanced data generation capabilities.  
• Trillions: Quantum chemistry applications could emerge, including drug discovery, battery simulation and materials 
discovery. 
These are the publicly known applications, according to Oxford Quantum Circuits (OQC). And as quantum computing capabilities 
improve, some of these use cases could emerge sooner than anticipated.  
Now, we’ll tackle the core concepts… 
Qubits 
Qubits (quantum bits) are a quantum computer’s basic unit of information. They can exhibit quantum properties such as 
superposition and entanglement (which we elaborate on below) — properties fundamental to quantum computing. 
Quantum gates manipulate the state of qubits, much like logic gates manipulate bits in classical computers. Despite the name, a 
quantum gate is not a physical object, but rather an operation applied to a quantum system that changes its state. 
From physical to logical qubits 
Physical qubits are the foundation of a quantum computer’s hardware. However, they are highly susceptible to error. To address 
this, multiple physical qubits can be structured together to encode a logical qubit, allowing errors to be detected and corrected

1 Schneider, J., & Smalley, I. (2025, June 10). What is quantum computing? IBM. 
I NSTI TUTE
Accessible version

### Page 2

2 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

and making the stored quantum information more reliable. In recent years, according to BofA Global Research, logical qubits 
have become a greater focus for quantum computing companies. 
Entanglement 
Entanglement is a condition where two or more qubits become linked so that their states are correlated, regardless of distance 
(Exhibit 1). It allows quantum computers to process information in ways that can be difficult or impossible for classical 
computers. 
So, how does it work? Suppose we have two photons that have opposite polarizations (the orientation of their light waves). 
Before measurement, neither has a definite polarization. When one is measured as horizontally polarized, the other will be 
vertically polarized when measured in the same basis, wherever it is. When measured separately, they appear to be random, but 
when compared, they are correlated (Exhibit 2). 
The challenges 
Once created, entanglement must be protected from decoherence (where interactions with the environment destroy quantum 
correlations). Maintaining entanglement over long distances is another challenge. Photons traveling through optical fiber can be 
absorbed or scattered, making long-distance quantum communication difficult. 2 Quantum repeaters aim to overcome this 
challenge by extending entanglement across a series of shorter links. 
Exhibit 1: Entanglement is a state where at least two qubits become 
correlated 
An infographic of entangled qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 2: Measuring one qubit in an entangled pair instantly 
determines the state of the other, no matter the distance 
An example of what entanglement means for qubit measurement

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Superposition 
Unlike a classical bit, which can be one of two states (0 or 1), superposition allows a quantum system to exist in multiple states 
or configurations at the same time (Exhibit 3). For example, a qubit can exist in the 0 state, the 1 state or any state that is a 
combination of 0 and 1.3  
A quantum system can maintain a superposition until it is observed or measured. Once measured, its superposition collapses into 
a single state. Another way of thinking about superposition is to imagine a sphere with the north pole representing zero and the 
south pole representing one. Other points on the surface of the sphere represent different superposition states. This allows us 
to: 1) represent complex forms of information; and 2) simulate atoms in a computer. 
Schrödinger’s cat 
In 1935, physicist Erwin Schrödinger proposed a thought experiment to illustrate the strange implications of quantum 
mechanics. In this scenario, a cat, a radioactive substance that decays very slowly, and a Geiger counter are put together in a 
sealed box. According to quantum mechanics, the system can be described as a superposition in which the cat is both alive and 
dead until the box is opened. Once the box is opened and the system is observed, the superposition collapses into a single 
outcome.

2 Rehman, M. (2026, May 11). What is Quantum Entanglement? Quantum Insider. 
3 Microsoft. (n.d.). Superposition.

### Page 3

21 October 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

Enabling parallel computation versus classical computing 
Superposition is a key attribute of quantum computing that allows for quantum parallelism, or the ability to perform multiple 
computations at the same time. Classical bits can only be in one of two states, 0 or 1. In contrast, a qubit can be in a 
superposition of 0 and 1, allowing a quantum computer to process multiple possible qubit states at once.4  
Exhibit 3: Superposition is the condition where a qubit can exist in a superposition of 0 and 1 
Illustrating the power of superposition

Source: BofA Global Research 
Note: A Bloch sphere is a geometric representation of qubit states. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Error rates and fidelity 
Fidelity is a measure of how closely a quantum state or operation matches what was intended (Exhibit 4). A higher fidelity 
generally means fewer errors. 
Physical qubits are prone to errors. One way to address this is to combine multiple physical qubits to create a more reliable 
logical qubit. Quantum error correction (QEC) allows errors in the physical qubits to be detected and corrected, helping protect 
the information stored in the logical qubit.

4 Ibid.

### Page 4

4 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 4: Fidelity measures how accurately a qubit retains its intended quantum state during computation 
Infographic showing the implications of high versus low fidelity

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
How does quantum error correction work? 
At its simplest, QEC relies on redundancy, or storing information across multiple qubits so that errors can be detected and 
corrected.  
Consider an example: suppose someone wants to send a message represented by 0. Instead of sending a single 0, the message 
could be repeated three times as 000, known as a codeword. If one bit were accidentally flipped from 0 to 1 during transmission, 
the recipient might receive 010. Because two of the three bits are still 0, the recipient could infer that the intended message 
was 000. 
However, if two bits flipped, producing 110 or 011, the recipient could infer the wrong message. If all three flipped to 111, the 
recipient would have no way to know from the received codeword that errors had occurred.5 This approach is known as a 
repetition code and illustrates how redundancy can help protect information from errors.  
Repetition codes are a simple form of error correction that can address bit-flip errors, where a 0 changes to a 1 or vice versa. 
More sophisticated QEC codes are needed to address other quantum errors, such as phase flips.6 Shor’s code, developed by 
American theoretical computer scientist Peter Shor, uses nine physical qubits to encode one logical qubit and can correct one 
bit-flip error or one phase flip error, but it can’t correct both types of errors at the same time. Meanwhile, Steane’s seven-qubit 
code, developed by Oxford physicist Andrew Steane, can also correct both types of errors. It has the advantage of being fault-
tolerant, meaning the error-correction process itself does not introduce additional errors.7 
At what error level might we start seeing quantum advantage? 
Some classical computing systems today operate with extremely high reliability, often described as having "17 nines" of fidelity 
(99.99999999999999999%). By comparison, quantum computers currently achieve fidelity rates of around 99.9%. Gerald 
Mullally, CEO of OQC, has argued that quantum systems would need to add roughly three more nines of fidelity to achieve 
commercial relevance, or quantum advantage. 
Noise and decoherence 
Noise refers to unwanted disturbances that affect quantum systems and can lead to errors in computations (Exhibit 5). It can 
come from sources such as interactions with the environment, electromagnetic interference and temperature fluctuations. 
Coherence is a quantum system’s ability to maintain its quantum properties, including superposition and entanglement. When 
coherence is lost through interactions with the environment, the process is known as “decoherence,” causing the system to 
behave more classically (Exhibit 6).8

5 Microsoft Ignite. (n.d.) Introduction to quantum error correction. 
6 Ibid. 
7 Ibid. 
8 Quandela. (n.d.) Quantum Decoherence.

### Page 5

21 October 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

To fully control qubits for calculations and information transfer, they need to be carefully protected from their surroundings. 
External factors like noise, energy, changes in Earth’s magnetic field, radiation from Wi-Fi or mobile phones or cosmic rays can 
interfere with the process. Even small disturbances can lead to decoherence, making it difficult to develop large-scale, fault-
tolerant quantum computers.9 
According to QuEra Computing, quantum noise can be mitigated through physical isolation of qubits, the development of more 
precise control techniques and quantum error correction, which helps detect and correct errors in physical qubits.  
Exhibit 5: Noise refers to unwanted disturbances, e.g., interactions 
with the environment, that lead to errors in quantum computation 
An infographic explaining quantum noise

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 6: Decoherence is when qubits lose their key quantum 
properties, superposition and entanglement 
An infographic of decoherence

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Quantum processing unit (QPU) 
Referred to as the brain of the quantum computer, the QPU is the core hardware where qubits reside and where quantum 
computations take place. QPUs rely on quantum behaviors like superposition and entanglement to tackle problems in areas such 
as cryptography, simulation and optimization. Just as a CPU (central processing unit) in classical computing contains multiple 
components, a QPU includes quantum gates (operations that manipulate qubits in a controlled way, similar to logic gates in 
classical computing) and interconnects, which enable communication between qubits and other quantum processors.10 
Next, we’ll look at broader computing architecture… 
Heterogeneous computing  
Heterogeneous computing refers to a future computing landscape likely combining classical and quantum computing, potentially 
unlocking more powerful, faster and cheaper compute and helping solve problems that classical computers cannot. According to 
BofA Global Research, AI and quantum computing could also converge and complement each other, enabling more 
transformative use cases. 
Quantum modality  
A quantum modality refers to the type of physical technology used to create and manipulate qubits. We discuss the modalities in 
greater depth in the following section. 
Lastly, we outline qubit technologies… 
Neutral atom qubit 
A quantum modality that uses individual atoms with no electrical charge as qubits (Exhibit 7). The atoms are held in place using 
focused lasers known as optical tweezers. Neutral atom qubit systems do not require atoms to be ionized, which means fewer 
energy constraints and potentially simpler cooling requirements. Atoms such as rubidium or cesium are typically used. The qubits 
are manipulated by changing the energy levels or positions of the trapped atoms using laser pulses.

9 QuEra. (n.d.) Quantum Noise. 
10 SpinQ. (2025, January 16). What does a Quantum Computer Look Like? Explain Key Parts.

### Page 6

6 21 October 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Exhibit 7: A quantum modality where atoms with a net electrical charge of zero are the foundation for qubits 
Key features of neutral atoms

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Photonic qubit 
A quantum modality that uses particles of light, known as photons, to carry quantum information (Exhibit 8).11 Optical 
components such as beam splitters, which divide light along different paths, and phase shifters, which change the phase of light, 
can be used to perform quantum operations. Photonic quantum computers can use integrated photonic chips to manipulate 
photons and enable quantum logic. 
Exhibit 8: A quantum modality relying on the quantum properties of light 
Key features of photonic qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

11 Moore, J. (2025, March 28). The 6 different types of quantum computing technology. TechTarget.

### Page 7

21 October 2026    7

I NSTI TUTE

Spin qubit 
A quantum modality that uses the intrinsic spin of electrons or nuclei in solid-state materials, such as silicon or diamond. These 
spins can be manipulated using magnetic or electric fields, and their states are read using optical or charge-sensing techniques. 
Spin qubits are particularly appealing because they can leverage existing CMOS (complementary metal-oxide semiconductor; a 
technology to build processors) manufacturing infrastructure, making it easier to integrate them with conventional processors. 
Superconducting qubit 
A quantum modality in which electronic circuits create qubits. They are built using Josephson junctions, where two 
superconductors are separated by a thin insulating layer. Microwave pulses manipulate the qubits’ quantum states to run 
computations.12  
Specifically, a resistance-free current oscillates back and forth around a quantum loop. An injected microwave signal excites the 
current into superposition states.13 To enable this process, superconducting materials are cooled to near absolute zero (around 
10-20 millikelvin) using dilution refrigerators, helping reduce thermal noise and maintain coherence. 
Topological qubit 
A quantum modality that aims to encode quantum information using topological properties rather than relying only on individual 
particle states (Exhibit 9). One way to think about this is through the paths certain quasiparticles take as they move around one 
another, creating “braids” in space-time. Because the information can depend on the overall pattern of these braids rather than 
small details of their paths, the approach is designed to be more resilient to certain disturbances. 
Exhibit 9: Resilient to small perturbations but requires special quasiparticles not yet been conclusively demonstrated in nature 
An infographic of topological qubits

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Trapped-ion qubit  
A quantum modality in which ions (electrically charged atoms) are held in place using electromagnetic fields. These ions act as 
the qubits and are controlled by the system with tightly focused laser beams.14 The qubits are held inside ultra-high vacuum 
(UHV) chambers.

有其他聯邦 、州判決仍為股價壓力； 2)波士頓科學
受資安事件影響生產宣告無法達成財測 ，HRS的左

心耳封堵術 (LAAC) 指南適用 WATCHMAN、 亞培
Amulet，可同時做消融+LAAC，但LAAC不能完全
替代口服抗凝血藥物 (OAC)對預防中風的效用 ，仍
為二線，等同喜憂參半 。另受自付成本增加 ，預期
擇期、外科手術需求疲軟延伸至年底 ，機器人手術
成長率區間接近 14.5%，下半年低於上半年 ，將以
擴大翻新系統的布署降低門檻及增加門診手術需求 ，
擴大目標市場 ，但必要醫療手術需求健康 ，心血管
類手術下半年仍成長，醫療器材基本面喜憂參半。
《星級獎金與醫療成本控管力使長期獲利分化》
每月聯邦醫療保險優勢計畫投保人數成長趨勢持續 ，
CMS預計於2026年10月中公布 2027年醫療優勢計
畫(MA)星級評等，因評級變嚴將影響醫保公司會員
留存率、競爭力及2028年品質獎金收入不確定性增
加，低於4星級業者面臨品質獎金減少與長期獲利承
壓風險。另無意外法案獨立爭議解決 (IDR)仲裁成本
持續推升商業保險理賠支出 ，增加2027年雇主團體
及ACA市場保費定價壓力 。管理式醫療業者今年展

現利潤優先於市占率、砍不賺錢計畫/人數拉抬利潤
率，但明年若保費調漲幅度不及醫療成本增速 ，恐
推升醫療損失率 、壓縮承保利潤 。後續獲利關鍵在
於星級評等 、保費調整能力及理賠成本控管 ，業者
間的長期獲利表現可能進一步分化。
2

《地緣風險變數使油價短期仍偏上》
OPEC+決議11月產出配額維持 9月規模，預期目前
配額將持續至今年年底 ，下一輪恢復最後一套 200
萬桶/日的額外減產措施將在明年視市況調整。依市
場推估，中東區域海運原油及成品油運輸出口量9月
底曾回至接近九成 ，受荷姆茲海峽油輪襲擊事件增
加，伊朗革命衛隊預告管理範圍擴大 ，使原油運輸
開始下降 ，但曼德海峽因沙國駐葉門大使稱已清除
胡塞武裝勢力 ，預期曼德海峽運輸量將不受影響 。
但受轉運延長運輸時間 、避開受制裁的原油採購 ，
加上搶補低庫存 ，市場追逐現貨使布油呈現大幅現

貨溢價，七國集團(G7)雖計畫在未來四個月內釋放
總計原油與柴油戰略儲備各 0.5億桶，總計1億桶，
但預計佔每天原油用量低 ，僅短期壓抑油價 ，主要
重點仍在美伊談判、美國向中東增兵、伊朗襲船，
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望

原物料 中立 受供應下降可能性增，較看好銅價，但留意銅關稅政策。

《天然氣價格：美持平或略低於去年 ，歐洲偏上行 》
美國鑽井活動緩升 ，增加較多在天然氣 ，隨美國新
的液化天然氣出口工廠上線 ，逐漸改善管道瓶頸 ，
天然氣 10月庫存僅高於五年均值 2%，注入期可持
續增至11月中，預估今年冬天暖氣度日數將持平去
年 ， 並 比 過 去 五 個 冬 季 的 均 值 低1% ， 預估美國
4Q26天然氣價格將因季節性走升，但持平或略低於
去年。卡達能源LNG船已出口 ，但非往歐洲 ，歐洲
儲氣量雖拉升 ，但僅達 70%，遠低於過往 ，TTF價
格須優於亞洲以吸引運往亞洲的 LNG轉往歐洲 ，加

上中東地緣緊張，預期4Q26 TTF價格仍有上行風險。
重點摘要
• 天然氣：美國天然氣出口增
加，庫存增速下降 ，使4Q26
美國天然氣價格持平或略低
於去年同期；歐洲因庫存偏
低的回補需求將使天然氣價
格偏上行。

《釋儲及放寬制裁增加柴油恐使煉油利潤見頂》
受歐美老舊煉油廠關廠 ，煉油產能因俄烏 、中東戰
事受損，估計全球煉油廠開工率年減近 800萬桶/日，
加上中、俄禁止柴油出口 ，使柴油供應比原油更緊
張。美國柴油庫存低於五年區間下緣 ，柴油價格創
高，因美未禁止柴油出口 ，柴油利潤保持強勁 。但
美協調G7國家釋出柴油儲備為期四個月 ，達成美俄

柴油供應協議增加供給 ，雖粗略換算為短期每日供
給增加不到全球 1%，預期對柴油價格壓力影響僅
5%內，明年隨設備漸修復、地緣風險降低後，才緩
降，但美國超額柴油利潤恐見頂。
《4Q26能源類股較看好綜合油企》
預估美綜合油企 4Q26受惠油價高於預期 、LNG出
口增加，柴油高裂解價差 ，但進入汽油需求淡季 ，
獲利季比下滑 ，但年比仍大幅成長 ，現金流仍強大 ，
維持資本紀律及股東回報 。雖成品油供應仍較原油
吃緊，但煉油商超額利潤恐見頂 ，較看好綜合油企 。

《預期4Q26商品化學利潤承壓，特化獲利持穩》
美國ISM 9月製造業PMI為54.5，連續第9個月位於
擴張區間；新訂單升至55.3，較8月53.7改善，化學
產品亦列入擴張產業 ，顯示美國化學品下游需求並
沒有陷入衰退 ，但化學製造業產能利用率仍低於
80%。受油價上漲推升進料價格 ，轉嫁化學產品價
格上漲 ，但漲幅不及成本上揚 ，壓縮利潤 ，預期
3Q26獲利低於2Q26，4Q26也低於3Q26，但基期
因素仍較去年大幅成長 ，但且因進料來源不同 ，北
美廠因較低成本優勢 。就塗料，其股價與利率呈負
相關，利率升股價下跌 ，雖受惠定價力 、室內裝修
與新成屋交屋塗裝及市占率增加 ，但因氣候影響戶
外漆銷售及 DIY市場疲軟 ，Q4獲利較 Q3下滑 ，但

YOY呈中個位數成長 。工業氣體受惠定價力及合約
保護，4Q26獲利年增率呈現中高個位數成長。

《預期金價短期不利因素多，但中期緩升》
近月金價歷經 美國通膨及經濟成長 ，美國FED採取
預防管理式升息一碼 ，認為年底前仍有升息一碼空
間，但受就業降溫及通膨低於預期 ，市場降低10月
升息預期 ，加上法國國債被拋售蔓延全球債市使美
長期公債殖利率及美元走強 、黃金淨多頭部位下降 ，
壓低本月金價下跌 ，也跌破季線 。預期短線金價受
債券殖利率飆升 、實質利率走升 及美元走強 相對不
利金價走勢 ，震盪放大。但若美債收益率回落 、擔
憂債務可持續性 、分散交易對手風險及多元化資產
配置，中國人行連續23個月購金，並加大購金步伐 ，
3Q26黃金ETF呈現創紀錄淨買超 ，預期3800-4000
美元有撐，維持金價中期緩步上行。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026/10/7，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10/12

[台湾]～生活必需品を中心にインフレ加速も、輸出・入額ともに月次ベースで過去最高を更新している～ 
７日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋2.73％となり、前月（同＋2.04％）から加速
して 2024 年３月以来の高い伸びとなった。前月比も＋0.44％と前月（同▲0.01％）から上昇に転
じており、 原油価格の再上昇を受けてエネルギー価格が上昇しているほか、 天候不順の影響により
生鮮品をはじめとする食料品価格も上昇するなど、 生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が強まってい
る。 なお、 野菜 ・ 果物とエネルギーを除いたコアインフレ率は前年同月比＋2.52％となり、 前月 （同
＋2.30％）から加速して７ヵ月ぶりの高い伸びとな った。前月比も＋0.06％と前月（同▲0.15％）
から２ヵ月ぶりの上昇に転じており、 エネルギー価格の上昇による輸送コストの押し上げを反映し
て財価格に上昇圧力がかかる一方、 景気の先行き不透明感を反映してサービス物価は下落基調にあ
るなど、物価の動きにばらつきが生じている。 
８日に発表された９月の輸出額は前年同月比＋60.9％となり、前月（同＋41.0％）から伸びが加
速した。 前月比も＋7.5％と前月 （同＋10.4％） に続いて２ヵ月連続で拡大しており、 持ち直しの動
きを強めるとともに、単月ベースの輸出額は過去最高を更新した。財別では、主力の輸出財である
半導体など電子部品関連や電気機械関連で拡大が続いているほか、 化学製品関連も堅調な動きをみ
せるとともに、商品市況の再上昇を反映して鉱物資源関連の輸出額も押し上げられた。国・地域別
では、中国本土向けが頭打ちとなり、日本向けも下振れする一方、米国向けや欧州向けが堅調に推
移しているほか、ASEAN などアジア新興国向けにも旺盛な動きがみられる。一方、輸入額は前年同

### Page 2

2/4 
月比＋51.7％となり、 前月 （同＋44.3％） から伸びが加速した。 前月比も＋7.7％と前月 （同＋6.4％）
に続いて２ヵ月連続で拡大しており、 持ち直しの動きを強めるとともに、 輸出額と同様に単月ベー
スの輸入額も過去最高を更新した。 財別では、 輸出の堅調さを反映して機械製品関連の輸入が堅調
な動きをみせるとともに、プラスチック関連など素材・部材関連の輸入も底堅いほか、金をはじめ
とする貴金属関連の輸 入が大幅に拡大したことも輸入額を押し上げた。その結果、貿易収支は＋
236.30 億ドルと前月 （＋222.98 億ドル） から黒字幅が拡大して、 月次ベースで過去最高となった。

[タイ]～エネルギー物価の上昇がインフレ圧力を強める一方、住宅価格やサービス物価に不透明感～ 
６日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋2.82％となり、 中銀の目標レンジ （２±１％）
の範囲内で推移しているものの、 前月 （同＋2.53％） から加速して４ヵ月ぶりの高い伸びとなった。
ただし、前月比は＋0.25％と前月（同＋0.56％）から上昇ペースが鈍化しており、原油価格の再上
昇を反映してエネルギー価格の上昇が続く一方、 穀物や生鮮品など食料品価格の上昇ペースは鈍化
するなど、 生活必需品の物価動向にはばらつきがみられる。食料品とエネルギーを除いたコアイン
フレ率は前年同月比＋1.50％となり、引き続き目標レンジ内で推移しているものの、前月（同＋
1.44％） から加速して2023 年５月以来の高い伸びとなった。 前月比は＋0.01％と前月 （同＋0.20％）
から上昇ペースが鈍化しており、 エネルギー価格の上昇が輸送コストを押し上げるとともに、 バー
ツ安に伴う輸入物価の上昇も重なり、幅広い財価格に上昇圧力がかかっている。一方、住宅価格が
下振れしたほか、 景気の先行き不透明感の高まりがサービス物価の重しとなるなど、 物価を巡る動
きにはばらつきが生じている。

[フィリピン]～生活必需品のみならず、幅広くインフレ圧力が強まり、インフレ率は再び加速している～ 
６日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋7.2％となり、６ヵ月連続で中銀の目標レン
ジ （２～４％） を上回る推移が続くとともに、 前月 （同＋6.1％） から加速して５ヵ月ぶりの高い伸
びとなった。前月比も＋0.95％と前月（同＋0.59％）から上昇ペースが加速しており、原油価格の
再上昇を反映してエネルギー価格が上昇しているうえ、 穀物や生鮮品など食料品価格も上昇するな
ど、 生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が強まったことが影響している。食料品とエネルギーを除い
たコアインフレ率も前年同月比＋4.7％となり、５ヵ月連続で目標レンジを上回るとともに、前月
（同＋4.1％）から加速して 2023 年 11 月以来の高い伸びとなった。前月比も＋0.59％と前月（同
＋0.37％） から上昇ペースが加速しており、 エネルギー価格の上昇を受けた輸送コストの増加に加
え、通貨ペソ安に伴う輸入物価の押し上げも重なり、財・サービスを問わず幅広く価格上昇圧力が
強まる動きがみられる。

[マレーシア]～半導体など電子部品関連の生産は引き続き旺盛だが、分野ごとのばらつきが鮮明に～ 
９日に発表された８月の鉱工業生産は前年同月比＋5.0％となり、前月（同＋4.7％）から伸びが
加速した。前月比も＋0.54％と前月（同▲0.10％）から４ヵ月ぶりの増加に転じており、これまで
の頭打ち傾向に変化の兆しがうかがえる。 分野別では、 原油や天然ガスをはじめとする鉱業部門の
生産が３ヵ月ぶりの増加に転じるなど持ち直しの動きをみせる一方、 製造業の生産は３ヵ月ぶりの
減少に転じるなど対照的な動きとなっている。 経済活動の動向に連動する傾向がある発電量は３ヵ
月連続で増加しており、 全体としては持ち直しの動きが続いている様子がうかがえる。 製造業のう
ち、 主力の輸出財である半導体など電子部品関連や電気機器関連の生産は旺盛な動きをみせる一方、
自動車など輸送用機械関連、 石油化学製品関連、 木製品や縫製品など軽工業関連の生産は力強さを
欠いており、分野ごとのばらつきが鮮明になっている。

3Q26 營收創同期新高，斬獲首筆 2奈米 AI設計案  
⚫ 受 ASIC與 MCU量產出貨帶動， 3Q26營收優於預期  
⚫ 2027年在 3大主線同步帶動下，預估營收成長達雙位數  
⚫ 成功取得 2奈米專案，奠定公司重要里程碑  
3Q26 營收季增 11%，優於公司前一季指引： 智原 3Q26 營收為 36.6
億元 (QoQ+11%，YoY+13%)，優於原先指引的季減個位數，季增主要
受 MP收入創新高帶動。其中 IP收入為 4.5億元 (QoQ+4%，YoY+8%)，
創單季新高； NRE收入為 3.1億元 (QoQ -31%，YoY -37%)，主要受專
案認列時間遞延影響； MP收入為 29.1億元 (QoQ+20%，YoY+25%)，
MCU 與 ASIC 出貨皆有成長。營業毛利為 15.6 億元 (QoQ+4%，
YoY+45%)，毛利率從上季的 45.2%下滑降至 42.5%，主要受產品組合
變化影響， MP收入比重增加稀釋整體毛利率。營業費用為 13.4億元
(QoQ+9%，YoY+30%)，費用增加受 7月集團年度調薪、員工分紅及
用人費用影響；營業利益為 2.1億元 (QoQ -21%，YoY+443%)；業外收
入為 2.7 億元，主要來自轉投資公司評價利益 2.6 億元。稅後淨利為
3.7億元，歸屬母公司稅後淨利為 2.8億元。單季 EPS為 1.07元。  
預估 4Q26 營收持平，毛利率回升至 45%:公司預估 4Q2 6 營收相較
3Q26 持平，其中 IP、NRE 收入將提高，MP收入則會減少，整體毛
利率約 45%。2026全年營收將超過 2022年的 130億元， 2027年營收
在三大主線同步成長帶動下，將以雙位數成長為目標。  
2 奈米專案取得重大進展，為智原取得其他先進製程專案奠定基礎：
智原成功取得美國客戶雲端 AI 晶片專案，成為在先進製程的重要里
程碑，客戶為追求更強大的效能，決定將原訂的 4奈米升級至 2奈米，
為智原首個採用 GAA 製程的設計案。智原負責包含晶片的前段和後
段設計，整體設計難度高，並首度引進 3D客製化 DRAM堆疊與 Hybrid 
Bonding 3D 先進封裝技術，專案 NRE金額達數千萬美元，合作晶圓
廠為三星，該案預計於 1H27依照進度開始認列 NRE收入，中長期將
挹注強勁的量產營收。成功取得該案有助於智原持續爭取更多大型客
戶先進製程 AI專案，目前智原已有其他 2nm/4nm 專案正在洽談。  
基於 2027年預估 EPS  7.15元*30倍 PER，給予目標價 215元，調升
評等至增加持股。風險因子 :ASIC與 MCU市場需求。

Document 759: 沙特双通道出口瓦解红海短缺叙事，中东原油溢价一个月从34美元_桶跌至6美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/沙特双通道出口瓦解红海短缺叙事，中东原油溢价一个月从34美元_桶跌至6美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

沙特东西输油管道的复通，重启红海延布港，与霍尔⽊兹海峡双线出⼝。据 Vortexa 数据，
沙特 10 ⽉⽇均出⼝量约为 650 万桶，与 9 ⽉的 660 万桶基本持平。衡量海湾地区现货市场强
弱的阿曼 - 迪拜价差单⽉暴跌逾 80% ，从 34 美元 / 桶骤降⾄ 6 美元。
摘要：
沙特阿拉伯正通过红海与霍尔⽊兹海峡双线并⾏的出⼝格局，打破外界对波斯湾原油供应中断
的悲观预期，令中东实货市场的溢价在短短⼀个⽉内⼤幅收窄。
船舶追踪机构初步数据显⽰，沙特 10 ⽉迄今的原油⽇出⼝量维持在接近战时⾼位的⽔平，整体
表现与 9 ⽉相当。与此同时，波斯湾地区其他主要产油国的出⼝也在向冲突前⽔平回归，供应紧
张的预期正在松动。
供应回暖已直接传导⾄实货价格。衡量海湾地区现货市场强弱的阿曼 - 迪拜原油价差，本⽉早些
时候跌⾄ 7 ⽉以来最低，本周⼆已降⾄约 6 美元 / 桶，⽽ 9 ⽉这⼀数字接近 34 美元 / 桶，单⽉降幅逾
80% 。
东西管道恢复，双通道打通供应瓶颈
沙特出⼝韧性的核⼼，在于东西输油管道的复通。该管道此前因袭击被迫停运，沙特出⼝⼀度

完全依赖经霍尔⽊兹海峡的东海岸航线。
管道恢复运营后，沙特重新激活了红海沿岸的延布港出⼝通道，使其得以同时运⽤两条出⼝路
线，在应对也⻔胡塞武装持续袭扰的同时，维持⾼位出货。
根据数据提供商 Vortexa 的数据，沙特 10 ⽉迄今经红海⽅向（主要为延布港）的⽇出⼝量达 250
万桶，⾼于 9 ⽉的 240 万桶；经波斯湾⽅向的⽇出⼝量为 400 万桶，较 9 ⽉的 420 万桶略有回落，
原因是该航线仍受到间歇性袭击⼲扰。
综合两线， Vortexa 统计的沙特 10 ⽉⽇均出⼝量约为 650 万桶，与 9 ⽉的 660 万桶基本持平。
不过，不同机构的数据存在明显分歧。 Tankertrackers.com 估计 10 ⽉迄今⽇出⼝量约为 699 万
桶，接近冲突前⽔平； Kpler 则估算仅为 540 万桶，低于 9 ⽉。
数据差异的主要原因在于，⼤量油轮已开始关闭卫星应答器以规避袭击⻛险，导致追踪难度上
升。这⼀信息盲区也给交易员和投资者评估区域供应形势带来了挑战。
买家重返延布港，欧洲现货补量同步推进

出⼝通道打通的同时，沙特国家⽯油公司沙特阿美正积极恢复延布港的买家客源。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠透露，阿美已要求部分亚洲买家从 11 ⽉起重新从延布港提货，此前这些买
家中断延布港提货已⻓达三个⽉，转⽽通过穿梭油轮和管道联络经埃及地中海港⼝取油，以规
避红海的胡塞威胁。
在欧洲市场，据彭博此前报道，阿美亦已通知此前遭取消合同的欧洲买家，可就本⽉及下⽉的
货物参与现货竞购，以弥补此前的缺量。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

图⽚
与此同时，沙特已向⽯油输出国组织（ OPEC ）报告， 9 ⽉产量提升了三分之⼀，达到超过 830
万桶 / ⽇的战时⾼位。沙特国内炼⼚通常每⽇消耗约 300 万桶，但吉赞炼油⼚（⽇处理能⼒ 40 万
桶）因胡塞袭击⽬前仍处于停⼯状态。
波斯湾整体供应趋向修复
沙特之外，波斯湾地区其他主要产油国的出⼝形势也在趋于正常化。
科威特完全依赖霍尔⽊兹海峡出⼝，其油企⾼层已表⽰出⼝已恢复⾄冲突前⽔平；伊拉克上⽉
出⼝量亦接近战前⽔平；阿联酋在油轮过境霍尔⽊兹海峡⽅⾯表现最为成功，据国际能源署数
据，其产量已升⾄历史⾼位。
多路供应同步改善，共同压低了中东实货市场的⻛险溢价。阿曼 - 迪拜价差从 9 ⽉的近 34 美元 / 桶
骤降⾄本周的约 6 美元 / 桶，表明此前市场对波斯湾供应的担忧已在相当程度上得到缓解。

然⽽，地区局势并未彻底平稳。胡塞武装对沙特机场及油⽓设施的袭击仍在持续，近期攻击已
实际影响到利雅得⾦融区的正常运转，并对沙特整体经济产⽣外溢压⼒。油轮信息透明度的下
降，也使市场对真实供应量的研判持续存在不确定性。
对沙特⽽⾔，在战时维持⾼油价收益的同时守住出⼝规模，仍是⼀道需要持续应对的双重考
验。

Document 762: 波音大跌！737产量目标推迟至明年，自由现金流指引大幅低于预期，商业航空承压｜财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/波音大跌！737产量目标推迟至明年，自由现金流指引大幅低于预期，商业航空承压｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

波⾳ Q3 营收 241.9 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 4% ，净亏损 1.16 亿美元，同⽐⼤幅减亏。 CEO 表⽰，
737 系列⻜机⽉产量达到 47 架的⽬标将推迟⾄ 2027 年初。公司预计 2026 年⾃由现⾦流 30 亿
美元，⼤幅低于市场预期的 55 亿美元。商业航空板块三季度运营亏损 6.97 亿美元，拖累整
体复苏表现。波⾳周⼆收跌 10.69% 。
摘要：
波⾳公司公布季度业绩，因产能爬坡进度落后及⾃由现⾦流前景不及预期，拖累股价⼤幅下
跌。
第三季度，波⾳净亏损 1.16 亿美元，较上年同期 54 亿美元的巨额亏损显著改善；总营收增⻓约
4% ⾄ 241.9 亿美元。
商业航空板块营收同⽐下滑约 3% ⾄ 107.2 亿美元，运营亏损达 6.97 亿美元，运营利润率为负
6.5% ，拖累整体复苏表现。波⾳将此归因于交付机型组合不理想以及供应链成本上升。季度业
绩同时受到约 7 亿美元司法部和解款项的⼀次性冲击。
波⾳⾸席执⾏官 Kelly Ortberg 周⼆表⽰，公司今年将⽆法完成 737 ⻜机的产能和交付⽬标，这是

其复苏进程的⼜⼀次挫折。
与此同时，波⾳将实现 100 亿美元⾃由现⾦流⽬标的时间推迟⾄ 2029 年，⽐市场普遍预期晚⼀
年，令投资者感到失望。
受上述消息影响，波⾳股价周⼆收跌 10.69% ，⾄ 168.18 美元，创 2025 年 4 ⽉ 3 ⽇以来最⼤单⽇跌
幅。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

Gabelli Funds 投资分析师 Tony Bancroft 表⽰，市场将惩罚任何现⾦流增⻓的延迟，尽管航空市
场⻓期需求强劲、竞争格局集中，波⾳的远期前景依然向好。
产能⽬标推迟，交付计划落空
波⾳⽬前将 737 ⻜机⽉产 47 架的⽬标推迟⾄ 2027 年初实现。
就在今年 5 ⽉，波⾳⾼管还表⽰将于今夏达到这⼀节奏。受此影响，波⾳⾸席财务官 Jay Malave
在周⼆与分析师召开的电话会议上确认，公司今年将⽆法完成 500 架⻜机的交付⽬标。
Ortberg 在电话会议上表⽰，机翼⽣产仍是主要瓶颈，但波⾳仍计划于 2027 年下半年将⽉产能提
升⾄ 52 架。
要实现这⼀⽬标，波⾳需要在位于华盛顿州埃弗雷特的新 737 ⽣产线上实现⽉产 5 架，并在堪萨
斯州威奇托的原 Spirit AeroSystems ⼯⼚提⾼机⾝产量。波⾳去年收购 Spirit ，部分原因正是为了
解决持续存在的制造质量缺陷问题。
Ortberg 表⽰，⽬前波⾳正在消化现有机⾝库存，⽣产速率维持在较低⽔平，从现在起必须提升

威奇托的产出以匹配 737 整机⽣产节奏。他同时强调： " 我们会保持理性和纪律，前景是明确
的。 "
现⾦流前景低于市场预期
波⾳预计 2026 年将产⽣约 30 亿美元⾃由现⾦流，低于华尔街普遍预期的 55 亿美元。据 Jefferies
分析师 Sheila Kahyaoglu 在周⼆研报中指出，差距主要来⾃交付数量减少以及资本投⼊增加。
就最新季度⽽⾔，波⾳实现⾃由现⾦流 4.64 亿美元，但今年前三季度合计仍消耗现⾦ 3.59 亿美
元，公司维持全年产⽣ 20 亿美元⾃由现⾦流的指引。
波⾳调整后每股亏损 34 美分，⾼于 LSEG 数据汇编的市场预期亏损 21 美分。截⾄本季度末，波
⾳净债务为 252 亿美元，较上季度末的 259 亿美元⼩幅下降，较⼀年前的 304 亿美元则明显收
窄。

### Page 3

商业航空承压，交付组合与成本双重挤压
商业航空板块是波⾳最重要的业务⽀柱，但第三季度表现不佳。该部⻔营收 107.2 亿美元，运营
亏损 6.97 亿美元，⽽上年同期亏损达 53.5 亿美元。利润率虽较去年同期的负 48.3% ⼤幅改善，但
仍维持负值。
波⾳将商业航空板块的承压归因于两个主要因素：⼀是机型交付组合不理想，即⾼利润机型占
⽐偏低；⼆是未来⽣产速率调整带来的更⾼预期成本，以及供应链估算成本上升。
第三季度商业航空共交付 161 架⻜机，较上年同期 160 架⼏乎持平，全年前三季度累计交付 475
架，同⽐增⻓约 8% 。
在⽣产进度⽅⾯，波⾳表⽰ 737 项⽬ " 持续向每⽉ 47 架的稳定产能⽬标推进 " 。⾸席执⾏官 Kelly
Ortberg 此前明确表⽰，在能够持续维持该稳定速率之前，⽣产线不会进⼀步提速。
787 项⽬⽬前维持每⽉ 8 架的产能⽔平。 777X 项⽬则继续推进 777-9 的适航认证⻜⾏测试，⾸架
交付⽬标维持在 2027 年。
需要注意的是， 787 项⽬仍存在⽣产挑战：⼀起供应商纠纷导致 777 货机及 767 机型（含 KC-46 空

中加油机）的机翼⽣产陷⼊停滞
Jay Malave 表⽰，机翼供应已恢复，但此次中断的影响将在 " 未来⼏个季度 " 持续显现。此外，
777-9 客机预计于 2027 年中完成监管认证，明年交付 10 ⾄ 15 架。
防务业务强劲，海军战机合同奠定⻓期增⻓基础
防务、空间与安全板块成为本季度亮点，营收同⽐增⻓ 15% ⾄ 79.4 亿美元，运营利润率从上年
同期的 1.7% 跃升⾄ 6.2% ，运营利润达 4.92 亿美元，同⽐增幅⾼达 332% 。
利润率提升部分得益于出售 HRL 实验室带来的 2.52 亿美元收益，以及运营效率的整体改善。
战略层⾯，波⾳本季度赢得美国海军 F/A-XX 第六代战⽃机项⽬，将负责设计、制造并交付这款
下⼀代舰载战⽃机。该项⽬尚未纳⼊积压订单，⽬前仍待完成来源选择与评估审查流程。这⼀
合同承接了波⾳ 2025 年赢得空军战⽃机⼤单的势头，进⼀步巩固其在军⽤航空领域的战略地
位。
此外，防务板块本季度还获得⼀份为期七年的 PAC-3 导引头扩⼤⽣产合同。防务、空间与安全板

块积压订单增⾄历史新⾼的 1,010 亿美元，其中来⾃美国以外客⼾的订单占⽐达 23% 。

### Page 4

Document 768: 消費者物価指数（全国・2026年9月） ～財を中心に物価上昇圧力強まる。＋３％台も視野～

Source PDF: all report pdf/消費者物価指数（全国・2026年9月） ～財を中心に物価上昇圧力強まる。＋３％台も視野～.pdf

Date: 2026-10-23

Economic Indicators

消費者物価指数（全国・2026 年 9 月） 
～財を中心に物価上昇圧力強まる。＋３％台も視野～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

25 年12 月以来の＋２％台。コアコアの上昇率も明確に拡大 
本日総務省から発表された26年９月の全国消費者物価指数では、生鮮食品を除く総合（ＣＰＩ
コア）が前年比＋2.1％と、8 月の＋1.7％から上昇率が0.4％Pt拡大した。市場予想通りの結果で
ありサプライズはない。＋２％台は 25年 12月以来のこと。また、季節調整済み前月比でも＋
0.5％と、８月の＋0.1％から伸びを大きく高めており、足元では物価上昇ペースの強まりがみら
れる。

(出所）総務省統計局「消費者物価指数」
全国 東京都区部

### Page 2

2/4 
は前年比＋2.4％と、8月の＋1.9％から上昇率が0.5％Pt拡大し、季節調整済み前月比も＋0.5％
と高い伸びとなった。米国型コア（食料及びエネルギーを除く総合）も前年比＋2.2％と、8月の
＋1.6％から大きく上昇している。ＣＰＩコアだけでなく、食料やエネルギーの影響を除いた指数
でも上昇率が高まっており、物価上昇圧力が強まっていることを示す結果といえる。 
東京都区部での水道料金上昇（前年の基本料無償化の裏）や第一子保育料無償化要因の剥落、
宿泊料の上昇（前年の下落の裏＋シルバーウィーク５連休）といった一時的な要因も含まれてい
ることには注意が必要だが、こうした一時的な要因を割り引いたとしても、全体的に強い動きと
なっている。米類の下落幅が大きく拡大するなかでも生鮮食品を除く食料は上昇率を高めたほ
か、日用品では二桁の上昇が続き、家庭用耐久財や教養娯楽用耐久財でも上昇率が加速した。先
に公表されていた９月の東京都区部でみられた財価格の強さが、全国でも確認された形である。 
26 年前半には食料品価格の鈍化を主因として物価上昇率に落ち着きがみられたが、７月以降は
日用品を中心に値上げの動きが再び広がっている。９月は食料品や耐久財にも強さが広がったほ
か、サービスにも一部で上昇の広がりがみられた。一時的な押し上げ要因を含む点には注意が必
要だが、物価上昇圧力の裾野が徐々に広がりつつあることを示す結果と評価できる。

食料品、日用品、耐久財で値上げの動きが広がる 
電気・ガス代は、前月からほぼ変化がなかった（電気・都市ガス代の前年比寄与度：８月▲
0.10％Pt → ９月▲0.09％Pt）。政府は７～９月にかけて電気・ガス代補助を復活させたが、これ
がＣＰＩに反映されるのは８～10月分であり、この期間のエネルギー価格は抑制される。昨年同
時期にも支援は実施されているが、今年は昨年よりも規模が大きいため、前年比でも下押し要因
になっている。一方、補助が切れる11月以降については、既往のエネルギー価格上昇の影響が反
映されることも相まって、電気・ガス代の大幅な上昇が予想されることに注意が必要だ。 
生鮮食品を除く食料は前年比＋2.9％と、８月の＋2.7％から上昇率を高めた。米類は前年比
▲20.6％と、8月の▲15.7％から下落幅をさらに拡大させており、食料品価格を大きく押し下げて
いるが、それ以外の品目では上昇が目立った。品目別では、パンが前年比＋4.1％から＋5.0％、
食用油が＋14.6％から＋19.0％、調理食品が＋3.3％から＋4.2％へ伸びを高めた。飲料も＋6.0％
と高い伸びが続いている。米以外の食料品で値上げの動きが進んでいる。 
先行きについても、米価格の下落は当面の下押し要因となる一方、包装資材費や物流費、人件
費などを理由とした値上げの動きは続くとみられる。米価格の下落による押し下げを勘案して
も、生鮮食品を除く食料にはなお上昇圧力が残る可能性が高い。 
日用品でも値上げの動きが一段と強まった。家事用消耗品は前年比＋12.4％と、８月の＋
10.1％からさらに伸びを高め、家具・家事用品全体でも＋4.9％から＋6.5％へ上昇率が拡大し
た。品目別では、ティシュペーパーが＋18.3％、トイレットペーパーが＋17.8％、漂白剤が＋
21.0％と高い伸びとなっている。原材料費や包装資材費、物流費など既往のコスト増が小売価格
へ転嫁される動きは一段と鮮明になっている。 
耐久財の価格上昇も目立った。家庭用耐久財は前年比＋3.9％から＋6.2％、教養娯楽用耐久財

### Page 3

3/4 
も＋4.5％から＋7.4％へ伸びを高めた。品目別ではタブレット端末が＋21.3％から＋28.7％へ上
昇したほか、テレビや電子レンジなどでも伸びが高まっている。こうした動きには、ＡＩ関連需
要の拡大を背景とした半導体・メモリー価格の上昇に加え、円安による輸入コスト上昇が川下価
格へ波及している可能性がある。日用品に加えて耐久財にも値上げの動きが広がっている点は注
目される。 
サービス価格は前年比＋1.5％と、8 月の＋1.2％から伸びを高めた。一般サービスも＋1.3％か
ら＋1.7％へ上昇している。自動車保険料（任意）が前年比＋5.9％、外壁塗装費が＋7.5％となる
など、一部には価格上昇の広がりを示す動きもみられる。 
ただし、９月の上昇率拡大には一時的・制度的な要因も影響している。宿泊料は前年比▲1.4％
から＋4.6％へ大きく上昇しているが、これはシルバーウィークによる押し上げが大きかったとみ
られる。また、保育所保育料も▲21.6％から▲13.3％へ下落幅が縮小したが、これは、昨年９月
に東京都で始まった第一子保育料無償化による前年比の下押し効果が一巡した影響が大きい。 
このように、サービス価格には一部で上昇の広がりを示す動きがみられる一方、一時的・制度
的な押し上げ要因も含まれている。基調として伸びが高まっているかどうかについてはまだ何と
も言えず、今後の動きをしっかり確認する必要があるだろう。 
こうしたなか、コアコアも明確に上向いた。前述の通り、日銀版コアは前年比＋2.4％、米国型
コアも＋2.2％と、前月の＋1.9％、＋1.6％からそれぞれ加速した。米類の下落幅が拡大するなか
でも米以外の食料品では値上げの動きが進み、生鮮食品を除く食料は伸びを高めた。また、日用
品で高い伸びが続いたほか、家庭用耐久財や教養娯楽用耐久財でも上昇率が大きく高まるなど、
財価格では値上げの動きがより明確になっている。サービス価格の基調が加速しているかどうか
についてはなお見極めが必要だが、足元の物価上昇圧力は財を中心に強まっていると評価でき
る。

先行きは一段と上昇率が高まる可能性大 
先行き、ＣＰＩコアの上昇率はさらに高まる可能性が高いと予想している。これまでの資源価
格上昇によるコスト増は企業物価段階ですでに顕在化しており、今後も食料品や日用品、耐久財
など幅広い財で川下への価格転嫁が続く可能性が高い。 
食料品については、包装資材高や物流費、人件費などを理由とした値上げの動きは今後も続く
とみられる。米価格の下落による押し下げを勘案しても、米以外の食料品では高い上昇圧力が残
る可能性が高く、食料品全体としても上方向をみておく必要がある。 
また、政府による電気・ガス料金支援は10 月までＣＰＩを押し下げる一方、11 月以降はその効
果が剥落する。加えて、既往の燃料価格上昇は燃料費調整を通じて一定の時間差を伴って電気・
ガス料金に反映される。今後、エネルギー価格による押し上げが大きくなる可能性が高い。な
お、政府は2027年 1～3月使用分を中心に今冬も電気・ガス料金支援を行う方向で調整してい
る。支援規模は現時点で未定だが、前年にも支援が実施されていたことから、前年比でみた押し
下げ効果は限定的となる可能性がある。

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
さらに、中東情勢を巡る不透明感が残るなか、原油・燃料価格はなお高水準で推移している。
今後も高止まりが続けば、輸送費や包装資材、化学製品などを通じて企業のコスト負担が高ま
り、食料品や日用品など川下価格への波及が強まる可能性がある。また、これまでの原油高も今
後、燃料費調整を通じて電気・ガス料金へ波及するとみられ、物価上昇を長引かせる要因となる
点にも注意が必要だ。 
このように、既往のコスト上昇の川下への波及、企業の価格転嫁姿勢の強まり、電気・ガス代
補助の終了、燃料価格上昇が電気・ガス代へ遅れて反映されること、資源価格の高止まりリスク
などを踏まえると、先行きの物価はなお上振れの可能性を意識しておく必要がある。ＣＰＩコア
はこの先上昇率を高め、26 年末から27年初にかけて前年比＋３％を上回る展開になると予想して
いる。
